2091:    The extraction projector removes every declared jet below the stable threshold. Hence the stable matrix
2092: is block upper triangular with the displayed diagonal. Its entries come from finitely many derivatives of
2093: finite local analytic maps, so they are finite uniform constants. The spectrum of a finite block triangular
2094: matrix is the union of the spectra of its diagonal blocks.

2150:

2152:

2155: the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference

2160: moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
2161: third row.
2162:    If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
2163: the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
2164: L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is
2165:

2170: the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
2171: remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

2257: Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection

2260:    The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
2261: before reflection positivity is invoked.
2262: Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
2263: except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
2264: many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
2265: LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
2266: proves the uniform estimate.
2267:    The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
2268: formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

2346: Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
2347: and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher
2348: normal return, coarea logarithm, and finite Schur endpoint are simultaneously holomorphic. On the closed
2349: half-radius tube, their derivatives through structural order three and every fixed passive-source order have
2350: finite fixed-template suprema.
2351: 
2352: Proof. Each species is a finite composition of multiplication, inversion, exponential, logarithm, the
2353: finite-cell Karcher mean, and an inverse of one of the matrices
2354: 
2355:                                          I + DN P,          Hopt + B(A).
2356: 
2357: 
2358:                                                         35
2359: 
2360: The path-atlas theorem supplies a positive holomorphy radius for the first five maps. The uniform
2361: inequalities ∥DN P ∥ < 1 and Hopt ≥ 2I persist on a smaller positive tube, so the two inverses are
2362: holomorphic there by a Neumann series. There are only finitely many bulk and aligned-reflection
2363: templates. Taking the minimum of their radii gives ηcom > 0; Cauchy’s estimate on the closed half-radius
2364: tube gives the uniform derivative constants.

2366: on that closed common tube of the third derivatives of respectively the Wilson word, Haar logarithm,
2367: coarea logarithm, normal return, and finite Schur endpoint after their scalar and complete two-jet parts
2368: have been removed. Let Cinc be the largest rooted incidence number of those templates and let Cconn be
2369: the row sum of the exponential covariance connector of Corollary 4.14. All these constants are finite and
2370: uniform under the standing convention.

2346: Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
2347: and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher

3245: Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra

3249: Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
3250: lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
3251: engineering-dimension-five component.
3252: Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
3253: difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
3254: group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
3255: cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
3256: Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
3257: from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
3258: 
3259: Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
3260: rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
3261: dimension-five component is zero.
3262: Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
3263: tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
3264: an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
3265: sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
3266: Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
3267: required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
3268: while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
3269: complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

491: 2.3     Nonlinear forty-nine-path section
492: Lemma 2.8 (Finite-cell analytic atlas and uniform Cauchy bounds). Let P49 be the finite path atlas
493: for one retained cell and its finite aligned-reflection template family. There exist fixed radii 0 < η0 < η1 ,
494: uniform under the standing convention, such that the product exponential, multiplication, inversion,
495: logarithm, and Karcher-mean maps defining every template extend holomorphically from Tη0 to Tη1 .
496:    Write the canonically rescaled local block as
497: 
498:                              B(x) = Lx + N (x),       N (0) = 0,             DN (0) = 0.
499: 
500: For every exponential kernel rate 0 < m < mP and r = 2, 3, there is a finite constant
501: 
502:                                              CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ) < ∞
503: 
504: such that
505:                                     sup ∥Dr N (x)∥Km ≤ CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ),
506:                                    x∈Tη0
507: 
508: uniformly under the standing convention. The same constants, enlarged by a finite maximum, serve the
509: aligned-reflection templates.
510: Proof. For one retained cell the block depends on finitely many g coordinates. The exponential and
511: multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
512: neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
513: retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
514: Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
515: finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
516: complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
517: bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
518: order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
519:    The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
520: number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
521: finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
522: Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
523: depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.
